## Executive dashboard

**What we are building.** Give the tool a song and get back a preset for the Line 6 HX Stomp guitar pedal that sounds like the guitar on the recording, checked on the real pedal. It works by trying candidate presets, comparing the sound with the recording, and adjusting until they match.

**Project Health:** ▲ At Risk  
Reason: Nothing is blocked, but the main bet is in doubt. The first blind listening on real songs (five songs, the five best drive candidates against the separated guitar) drew 'so bad I can't even compare them'. That contradicts the open listens of 22 October that called the same kind of match 'pretty good', and a blind listen is the stronger evidence. Nothing computed from a recording sets the reverb: a pre-registered pilot found no agreeing signal, and the estimator built afterwards held on one song of two. The pass bars for the sealed drive test are proposed, not approved, so the next product-level milestone cannot be scored. [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)") was closed as superseded on 25 October (its model was already judged unusable). The open bet is a predictor of the new product chain (drive, equaliser, reverb); its training data is still being rendered, so it has no result.

**Technical Progress:** Advancing  
Reason: Since 23 October: a sealed 120-item test for the drive stage, a file that pins the song-match defaults to what the human has approved, a colour comparison on seven songs, a reverb pilot, a blind reverb estimator validated on 189 known reverbs, and a pre-registered confirmation. An overnight job has rendered 37,998 of about 40,000 chains for the product-chain predictor. Advancing is earned on tooling and on negative results that narrowed the product, not on matching quality: no new capability has been verified on real songs, and the one blind song test was negative.

**Product Readiness:** Prototype (stage 2 of 5)  
Reason: At the low end of Prototype. A script turns a separated song into a rebuilt clean guitar, finds a drive effect and its knobs, and writes a preset that plays on the Stomp, and bolt-on pilots show a light reverb and a fitted equaliser can be built. But the one blind listening of the drive-only matches was negative, so the demonstration is weaker than the open listens suggested. It is not Integrated: the steps are hand-run, the colour stage and the chain predictor are in no matcher, the reverb has no model, and the pedal check is manual. It is not Validated: no gate has been scored on a bar the human approved.  
Blocking the next stage: Integrated needs the product chain (drive, equaliser, reverb) predicted together from a song, run from one command, with the preset checked on the pedal. Today the drive match was rated badly blind, the reverb has no model and the chain predictor has no result. Validated also needs the [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") bars approved, the sealed set scored once, and a blind listen that goes the right way.

Readiness stages (the highest demonstrated is in bold): Foundation → **Prototype** → Integrated → Validated → Ready

**Summary:** The goal is a tool that turns a song into an HX Stomp preset that sounds like the recorded guitar. This week the first blind listening on real songs came back negative: the human said the drive-only matches were so poor they could not even be compared, although earlier open listens called them pretty good. The human then narrowed the product to a drive effect, an equaliser and a light reverb, with no amp, cabinet or delay. A light reverb is preferred by ear, but nothing measured from the recording picks it reliably: one song confirmed a reverb estimator and one did not. A sealed blind test for the drive stage exists, but its pass bars await the human's ruling. An overnight job is rendering training data for a chain predictor and has no result yet.

> **Biggest problem: Nothing measured from the recording reliably sets the drive knobs, and the first blind test of drive-only matches was negative** (High, Confirmed Problem)
>
> **What is wrong.** On 25 October the human heard, blind, the five best drive candidates for each of five songs against the separated guitar and said 'only 23 comes even close. That was pretty bad all around', then 'it's so bad I can't even compare them'. This is the first blind listening on real songs and it is negative (evidence class C as recorded by the lead: heard blind, answered aloud, not scored). It contradicts the open listen of 22 October that called the seven matches 'pretty good'. The dry-signal ablation then showed the winning drive model barely depends on the clean guitar fed to the search: against the plain rebuild, 5 to 6 of 7 songs keep the same model under each alternative input, one song changes model in every arm, and the distances are not comparable across inputs. The listening measure that ranks the candidates (AFx-Rep) has no ear validation against a separated guitar, and its validation session produced a verdict instead of ranks.  
> **Why it matters.** The product promises a preset that sounds like the recorded guitar, and the first blind listening on real songs said the current drive-only matches do not. Reverb, the next stage the human wants, has no usable signal either. Until a model tied to what the human hears beats a sealed baseline and passes a blind listen, the tool cannot know whether a candidate is closer, so more search or tuning would be steering by an instrument the ear has not validated. The open bet is a predictor trained on drive, equaliser and reverb chains; it is still rendering its data.  
> **What it prevents.** A drive-only match cannot be shown to beat anything the human would accept, and the ranking and knobs rest on a measure the ear has not validated on real songs. Without a signal that tracks what the human hears, neither search nor prediction can be tuned toward a better answer.  
> **What resolves it.** Predict the product chain with a model trained on chain-rendered audio and judge it against the sealed set and a blind listen, with bars the human approves first; rate the built dry-ablation packs; treat the stem verdict as a standing negative until a blind session goes the other way.  
> **Next step.** Read the chain predictor when training ends; the human rates the dry-ablation packs and rules on the [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") bars.

**Next milestone: [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)")** (In Progress). Show that the tool can name the right block and settings from the rendered output alone, without ever seeing the dry guitar track that made it.  
Readiness: 1 of 4 acceptance criteria evidenced; owner sign-off pending.  
What must happen: The human rules on the bars; one frozen method is scored once on the sealed set against the baselines; then the short blind listen (at least 30 decided trials, 6 repeats). The method to score is the chain predictor, which has no result yet.  
Blocked by: The human has not ruled on the bars. Scoring allows one attempt per frozen method, and the method that would be scored (the chain predictor) is still being trained. Limit: the 120 items share about 31 distinct playing windows.  
Next action: Human: rule on the bars. Then the lead launches one scoring run of a frozen method.

### Gate 3 bars are proposed, not approved, so the sealed test cannot be scored

**High** · Active Blocker

- **Problem.** A sealed test set exists: 120 single drive-block items rendered from the reserved third guitarist's recordings, hashes recorded, labels unread. Benchmark wrote the pass bars down before drawing it: right model first at least 0.50 and in the top three at least 0.70; Drive knob within 0.15 on at least 0.60 of right-model items (and 0.50 across the other free knobs); each bar beating every baseline by 0.15 or more (paired, exact test p below 0.05); then a short blind listen with at least 30 decided trials where the prediction wins at least 70 percent. The human has not ruled on any of them.
- **Impact.** The only product-level milestone cannot be scored, and the overnight predictor's numbers, when they arrive, will not count toward the gate. A set allows one scoring attempt per frozen method.
- **Cause.** Waiting on a human ruling (standing rule: pass bars need the human's approval before they take effect). A limit to weigh: the 120 items share about 31 non-overlapping 10-second windows of playing, so the effective sample is nearer 30 to 40 than 120.
- **Resolution.** The human approves, changes or rejects each bar; the lead records it in the human's words; benchmark scores once.
- **Owner.** the human rules; benchmark scores after the lead launches the run
- **Next step.** Human: rule on the [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") bars (see the action list).
- **Evidence.** [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") sealed set gate3-v1 note; [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") set sealed: 120 items; [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") bars registered (proposed, not approved); Decision log; Gate checklists

### Nothing measured from the recording reliably sets the drive knobs, and the first blind test of drive-only matches was negative

**High** · Confirmed Problem

- **Problem.** On 25 October the human heard, blind, the five best drive candidates for each of five songs against the separated guitar and said 'only 23 comes even close. That was pretty bad all around', then 'it's so bad I can't even compare them'. This is the first blind listening on real songs and it is negative (evidence class C as recorded by the lead: heard blind, answered aloud, not scored). It contradicts the open listen of 22 October that called the seven matches 'pretty good'. The dry-signal ablation then showed the winning drive model barely depends on the clean guitar fed to the search: against the plain rebuild, 5 to 6 of 7 songs keep the same model under each alternative input, one song changes model in every arm, and the distances are not comparable across inputs. The listening measure that ranks the candidates (AFx-Rep) has no ear validation against a separated guitar, and its validation session produced a verdict instead of ranks.
- **Impact.** A drive-only match cannot be shown to beat anything the human would accept, and the ranking and knobs rest on a measure the ear has not validated on real songs. Without a signal that tracks what the human hears, neither search nor prediction can be tuned toward a better answer.
- **Cause.** Not established. Candidates named in the notes: one drive effect standing in for a whole rig (the human has since moved to drive, equaliser and reverb), the rebuilt clean guitar being an approximation, the separated stem carrying leftovers from other instruments, and the measure not tracking the ear on real songs. None was isolated.
- **Resolution.** Predict the product chain with a model trained on chain-rendered audio and judge it against the sealed set and a blind listen, with bars the human approves first; rate the built dry-ablation packs; treat the stem verdict as a standing negative until a blind session goes the other way.
- **Owner.** learning (the chain predictor, running); benchmark scores once the human approves the bars; the human rates the dry-ablation packs

### Reverb has no model: nothing measured from the recording agrees with the listener, and a real tail is readable on only 2 of 7 stems

**High** · Confirmed Problem

- **Problem.** The human rejected a fixed reverb default and asked for a model. The pre-registered pilot (27 reverb candidates per song, blind ranks on six songs, the listener consistent: mean rank difference 0.33 against 2.67 by chance) found no agreement from any computable signal: the listening measure 0.05, tail statistics 0.04, the time-based regressors -0.09, dip fill -0.40 (opposite sign). A blind estimator built afterwards is accurate on known reverbs (decay ordering 36 of 40 triples, rank correlation 0.85 to 0.89 on five songs; mix 0.88 to 0.94) but cannot tell Room from Plate from Hall (43 percent against 33 by chance), and on the real stems it reads a real tail on only 2 of 7 (Mother 0.70 s, Territorial 0.90 s); the other five read the separator's leftover. The pre-registered confirmation held on Mother (named closest ranked 1 of 7, named furthest 7 of 7) and was not reproducible on Territorial (the human's repeat got ranks 4 and 1), so pooled it did not confirm.
- **Impact.** The product chain needs a reverb per song. The only evidence that reverb helps is open listening (a light reverb preferred on all seven songs); type and amount cannot be chosen from the stem on 5 of 7 songs, and Plate, the open favourite, was not preferred in the blind ranks (mean rank Room 3.9, Hall 4.0, Plate 4.4; ties frequent).
- **Cause.** Known in part: separator leftovers hide the tail on 5 of 7 stems and the human said not to work on the separator; the shared low and high cuts remove most of what tells reverb types apart; some clips are hard to rank (Territorial repeat inconsistent; the Cherub Rock section was unsuitable).
- **Resolution.** The human rules on 'estimate when readable, fall back otherwise'; the overnight reverb heads are compared with the estimator on the seven songs; more songs are ranked blind with the estimator's pick named in advance.
- **Owner.** tone-metrics (estimator); learning (reverb heads); the human rules on the model and ranks more songs
- **Next step.** Human: rule on the reverb model and choose another Cherub Rock section (see the action list).
- **Evidence.** [T230](../../board/TASKS.md#t230 "Reverb-model pilot (human 2026-10-25 \"yes\")") reverb-model pilot note; [T230](../../board/TASKS.md#t230 "Reverb-model pilot (human 2026-10-25 \"yes\")") pre-registered scoring: listener repeat consistency; [T231](../../board/TASKS.md#t231 "Blind reverb estimator") blind reverb estimator note; [T231](../../board/TASKS.md#t231 "Blind reverb estimator") reverb estimator validation rows; [T232](../../board/TASKS.md#t232 "Pre-registered confirmation of the T231 reverb estimator on Mother…") reverb estimator confirmation note; [T232](../../board/TASKS.md#t232 "Pre-registered confirmation of the T231 reverb estimator on Mother…") pre-registered confirmation scoring; Decision log

### The product-chain predictor is the open bet, and single-block predictors do not transfer to chains

**High** · Confirmed Problem

- **Problem.** The human set the product chain to drive, equaliser, reverb (no amp, cabinet or delay). The predictors built for other slots were trained on single blocks and fail inside chains: inside a finished chain the drive is named right 2 times in 100 and the amp 1 in 100, against 93 and 54 alone. A small chain-trained pilot reached 18 percent top-3 on the drive (chance 6). A larger job is rendering now: about 40,000 drive, optional equaliser and optional reverb chains, 15 percent from the human's own session recordings. At 2026-10-26 16:23 local it had 37,998 chains done (shard 96 of 105, 2 dropped); rendering stops by 20:00, then training. No result, no ledger row, nothing adopted.
- **Impact.** Until a chain predictor works, every song match is a single drive effect and the product cannot be called integrated. If the larger run also fails, the plan for predicting a chain needs a different method.
- **Cause.** Predictors and search were built drive first, and single-block data came before the chain check. Inside a chain, anything in front of a block hurts its predictor and a cabinet after it nearly erases it.
- **Resolution.** Read the new heads against held-out library files, the held-out part of the human's session and the seven songs; then, once the bars are approved, against the sealed [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") set; the human hears any adopted match blind.
- **Owner.** learning runs the job; benchmark scores; the human approves the method
- **Next step.** Let the render and training finish, then read the seven-song table.
- **Evidence.** [T233](../../board/TASKS.md#t233 "Product-chain predictor") product-chain overnight job script (in progress, no result); Decision log; Chain transfer test: slot predictors on 295 finished chains (drive 0.02, amp 0.01 exact model; measurement only); Chain transfer test note; Overnight slot-dataset results note

#### The effect-slot predictors are trained, not verified: weak on a new guitarist and failing inside chains

**High** · Confirmed Problem

- **Problem.** On held-out recordings of players seen in training (18 files from the two library players; the many test clips are cut from those files, so the effective sample is 18, not thousands) the predictors name the exact model about 54 percent of the time for amps (224 models), 76 percent for cabinets, 56 percent for delays, 75 percent for modulation, 47 percent for reverb and 82 percent for equalisers. On a player held out of training the same predictors drop to roughly 19 to 31 percent (amp), 32 to 35 (cabinet), 25 to 32 (delay), 47 to 60 (modulation), 31 to 39 (reverb) and 49 to 67 (equaliser). Inside a finished chain they fall to near chance. Knob accuracy is modest, and the 'constant mid-range guess scores about 0.3' baseline it is compared with was not re-measured. Scope note: the amp, cabinet, modulation and delay slots are out of scope while the product chain is drive, equaliser and reverb (human, 2026-10-25), so these numbers matter again only if the chain is widened.
- **Impact.** The human is a new player to these predictors, and so is any later user, and the product matches chains. As they stand, the slot predictors cannot shortlist anything in a chain matcher.
- **Cause.** Only two library guitarists and one session of the human's playing exist in the training pool. Drive showed the same gap until the 150,000-render dataset added more guitar variety (new-player model accuracy 33 to 47 percent became 62 to 75).
- **Resolution.** Train on chain-rendered audio first (the pilot), then add guitar variety: the human's calibrated notes, and another licensed library if one can be found. Re-measure with the same held-out-player split and on the chain test set.
- **Owner.** learning builds the data; the human decides which guitar material to add
- **Next step.** Read the chain training pilot; then propose which guitars to add and the render budget.
- **Evidence.** Overnight slot-dataset results note; Amp slot heads, held-out DI files; Cab slot heads, held-out DI files; Delay slot heads, held-out DI files; Equaliser slot heads, held-out DI files; State of the project, 2026-10-23 (with the corrections appended the same day); Chain transfer test: slot predictors on 295 finished chains (drive 0.02, amp 0.01 exact model; measurement only); Methodology audit, 2026-10-23 (independent, read-only; evidence classes, overstatements found)

#### No pedal measurement counts until the sampling defects are handled

**High** · Confirmed Problem

- **Problem.** The pedal verification found real defects: the audio stream over USB is not stable for about 0.7 s after it starts, amps carry sound over from the previous render, the pedal refuses four effect models in whole-preset writes, and some delay and reverb settings glide slowly when changed in place. Setting truth passed 20 of 40 items, repeatability 12 of 17. A longer pre-roll fixed the first problem, but a one-sample slip still happens about once in 90 renders. The human confirmed the pedal's blocks are editable after sessions.
- **Impact.** The pedal is the final judge of the preset. Until the slip is detected or re-taken, pedal numbers cannot be used as evidence, and earlier pedal agreement figures are understated in noise. [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)") (Native only) is not affected; [Gate 5](../../board/GATES.md#gate-5--beats-the-nearest-preset-on-real-songs-was-gate-3-then-gate-4-renumbered-2026-10-17-and-2026-10-18 "Gate 5 — beats the nearest preset on real songs (was Gate 3, then Gate 4; renumbered 2026-10-17 and 2026-10-18)") is.
- **Cause.** Known for each defect (stream start-up, amp state, device refusals, parameter glide). The remaining slip has no recorded cause.
- **Resolution.** Detect and re-take slipped renders, find why four models are refused, measure or avoid the parameter glide, then re-register the pedal rules (float32 read-back, an absolute -60 dB settle criterion, floors from rested renders) and re-run.
- **Owner.** stomp-engine; the lead schedules the re-run
- **Next step.** Detect or re-take slipped renders, then fix the refusals and the glide.
- **Evidence.** Pedal verification, sessions 1-3 on the old pre-roll (setting truth, repeatability, settle timing); Pedal verification, last full run on the longer pre-roll: setting truth 20 of 40; sentinel tripped on a real one-sample slip; Decision log; Task board

#### Blind evidence on real songs is one negative session, and the listener's consistency is mixed

**High** · Confirmed Problem

- **Problem.** The seven song matches and their fine-tunes were heard once, openly. The first blind listening on real songs (25 October) was negative, but it was answered aloud and not scored, so there is no agreement number for the listening measure. The listener was very consistent on the reverb pilot (mean rank difference 0.33 against 2.67 by chance, 6 of 6 within 1) and mixed on the confirmation (Mother within 1, Territorial not reproducible); across the earlier pair sessions the human's repeats agreed 7 of 12 times. The one gate run that was blind, sealed and pre-registered ([Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)")) was disowned by the human and is now closed as superseded. The score the lead admitted for the known-recording case is unapproved and at chance on hard same-performance pairs (10 of 25).
- **Impact.** The ear is the final judge, and an open listen can flatter a method. Every 'closer', 'same or better' and 'pretty good' in this report is directional, not a result; method choices made on those numbers (fine-tuning, calibration, chord lists) are not yet defensible.
- **Cause.** Blind sessions take preparation and the quick open listens were what the human asked for; meanwhile test settings became defaults by reuse (the final ranking by the listening measure, knob fine-tuning with the level knobs free) without an itemised approval, which the lead has acknowledged as its failure.
- **Resolution.** Run the blind validation of the listening measure against separated guitars (5 songs by 5 candidates on the same rebuilt dry, ranked blind with repeats; report rank agreement); ablate fine-tuning and calibration blind, one factor at a time; carry at least 6 repeats per session and report the listener's consistency first; seal [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") with bars before the chain pilot is read; record approvals one line per item.
- **Owner.** the human decides the design (decisions 2, 3, 6, 8); the lead prepares the packs; benchmark registers bars
- **Next step.** Score the stem-validation pack if the human wants the agreement number; rate the dry-ablation packs; carry at least 6 repeats per session and report the listener's consistency first.
- **Evidence.** Knob fine-tuning on the seven picks (distances); Calibrated-guitar re-run on seven songs (distances); DS-Sample chain tuning, blind ratings session (v6); Blind rating session v3: hard same-performance pairs (learned measure 18 of 25; admitted spectral measure 10 of 25); Blind rating session v5: the same experiment's own picks (learned measure 20 of 21); Blind rating session v6 repeat consistency (1 of 2; 7 of 12 across sessions); Methodology audit, 2026-10-23 (independent, read-only; evidence classes, overstatements found); Project analysis, 2026-10-23 (lead): verdict, alternatives, ten decisions for the human; Decision log; [T223](../../board/TASKS.md#t223 "Blind validation of AFx-Rep against separated stems") AFx-Rep stem-validation pack note (unscored); [T230](../../board/TASKS.md#t230 "Reverb-model pilot (human 2026-10-25 \"yes\")") pre-registered scoring: listener repeat consistency

#### The pedal and Native disagree on one cabinet setting

**High** · Confirmed Problem

- **Problem.** The cabinet's Early Reflections setting does not sound the same on the pedal and in Native.
- **Impact.** A preset found on the fast Native engine can sound different on the real pedal. At the value the search chose, the two engines' recordings cancel by only 3.6 dB (poor agreement); with the setting at 0 they cancel by 31 dB (close agreement).
- **Cause.** Not yet known. Candidates are an older algorithm in the pedal, a different parameter scale, or a delay-structure difference. Other parameters tested at their extremes agreed.
- **Resolution.** Find the cause, or measure every searchable parameter on both engines and let the search free only the parameters that agree.
- **Owner.** native-engine and stomp-engine; benchmark for the per-parameter audit
- **Next step.** Keep the setting at 0 for pedal presets and let the pedal check it (the accepted rule); the verification suites cover settings generally.
- **Evidence.** Pedal versus Native agreement on four presets; Decision log; Pedal versus Native agreement on firmware 3.80 (spot checks)

#### Native verification leaves recorded misses that are explained, not fixed

**Medium** · Confirmed Problem

- **Problem.** After three engine fixes (baked controls no longer sent, hosts that start in the other numeric mode are re-prepared, a state latch from the first amp loaded), repeatability is 216 of 216 and nothing unexplained remains. Two rules still miss as written: setting truth 116 of 120 and baked values 63 of 73. Seven misses are one-step float rounding in how Native lands a control value; seven are effect-loop and send blocks.
- **Impact.** None blocks [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)") (ruled acceptable on 2026-10-17). Native results produced before the fixes were made by the earlier engine and may differ by rounding up to about -21 dB on a few chains.
- **Cause.** Native's own arithmetic and the nature of input and output blocks.
- **Resolution.** Exclude those blocks from search pools (done), and re-state the rule with a one-step tolerance for the next version.
- **Owner.** native-engine; the lead owns the rule wording
- **Next step.** None needed before the second [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)") attempt.
- **Evidence.** Native setting check, setting truth: 116 of 120 pairs, rule 2b 63 of 73; Native setting check, repeatability, after the engine fixes: 216 of 216 renders within the line, worst -161.5 dB (the first run was 215 of 216, worst -97.4 dB); Native setting check, baked values: zero violations; Native host canary: 15 of 153 fresh hosts opened in numeric mode B; zero unexplained results; Native in-run sentinel catches a forced numeric-mode drift; Decision log

#### The software engine cannot stand in for every pedal effect

**Medium** · Risk

- **Problem.** Measurements show Native can stand in for the pedal on amps, cabinets, dynamics, equalisers, most drives, delays, Room, Plate, Spring and Chamber reverbs, Hall above 500 Hz, and the modulation effects. It cannot for Ganymede-type reverbs, Hall on bass-heavy material, Bitcrusher-type drives and the cabinet Early Reflections setting, and blocks that modulate on their own are left out of the search unless asked for.
- **Impact.** Presets that use those effects must be checked on the pedal, which is slower; the block library also has no measured fingerprints yet, so models are not yet chosen from measurements.
- **Cause.** The two engines run some effects differently, and Native's control steps limit fine settings (now 10 ms for delay time instead of 63 ms).
- **Resolution.** Keep the pedal as the checker for the listed effects; finish the block fingerprints and then model choice from the library.
- **Owner.** native-engine (fingerprints), search (model choice), benchmark (agreement measurements)
- **Next step.** Finish the block fingerprints (in progress), then model choice.
- **Evidence.** Native block library: 605 of 605 pedal models captured; Narrower control ranges honoured by Native (delay Time in 10 ms steps); Time-effect agreement measured with test tones (reverbs, modulation, delays); Gray Flanger and Deluxe Phaser agreement on 20 s tones; Pedal versus Native agreement on firmware 3.80 (spot checks)

#### Everything is verified on one computer, one pedal firmware, with a private helper

**Medium** · Risk

- **Problem.** Pedal control is verified on macOS and firmware 3.80 only, and the small helper program that talks to the pedal is intentionally not published.
- **Impact.** Other users on Windows or Linux may not be able to use pedal mode, and others cannot reproduce the pedal measurements.
- **Cause.** Only one machine is available, and the helper's source is withheld on the owner's decision.
- **Resolution.** Publish a wrapper or rewrite the helper to the documented protocol; test on other systems when machines exist.
- **Owner.** stomp-engine
- **Next step.** None needed before [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)").
- **Evidence.** Pedal control notes

#### The rebuilt clean guitar barely changes which drive wins, and nothing says it improves the match

**Medium** · Unknown/Unverified

- **Problem.** Songs have no clean guitar, so the matcher rebuilds one from transcribed notes. The dry-signal ablation (4 arms per song plus a level-only arm, 30 searches) found the winning drive model mostly unchanged by the input: against the plain rebuild, the envelope-imposed rebuild kept the model on 6 of 7 songs, the library clean guitar and the level-only arm on 5 of 7; DS-Sample changed in every arm and Satisfaction's plain pick is a dead tie. The distances are from different dry tracks and are not comparable, so they say nothing about which input is closer. The seven blind packs are built and unrated. The level convention is settled provisionally at the play-along level (-28.1 dBFS; bundle approval, 25 October).
- **Impact.** Either the rebuild adds little to the pick, or the measure cannot see its effect; the ear on the unrated packs is the only way to tell. Until then the rebuild and the calibration cannot be credited with a gain.
- **Cause.** Level and the drive amount are entangled, the rebuild ablation (same search, generic library clean guitar) was never run, and the calibrated run changed three things at once.
- **Resolution.** The human rates the seven dry-ablation packs (blind, sealed keys) before reading the picks table.
- **Owner.** the human rates; dry-signal reads the result
- **Next step.** Human: rate the dry-ablation packs.
- **Evidence.** Seven-song dry-track drive search (distances); Calibrated-guitar re-run on seven songs (distances); Mother without chord data (distance 0.629); Guitar calibration profile note; Basic Pitch settings sweep against the human's dry takes; Research note: the dry-signal strategy (rebuild, effect removal, separation, level); Methodology audit, 2026-10-23 (independent, read-only; evidence classes, overstatements found); [T224](../../board/TASKS.md#t224 "Dry-signal ablation (decision 4) + level-only ablation") dry-signal and level-only ablation note; [T224](../../board/TASKS.md#t224 "Dry-signal ablation (decision 4) + level-only ablation") dry ablation search rows (picks only)

#### Guitar separation quality on real songs is unknown

**Medium** · Unknown/Unverified

- **Problem.** Guitar separation has been scored only on artificial mixes: quality falls from 21.2 dB (guitar alone) to 7.3 dB with a second guitar in the mix. No real song has a quality score. Update 2026-10-23: seven real songs have been separated and heard, and the human compared each match with the separated guitar, but no separation quality number exists for real songs, and one stem lost about 20 dB of bass. Update 2026-10-25: the human said not to work on the separator ('The extracted samples are just fine'); the reverb estimator shows five of seven stems hide the tail under separator leftovers.
- **Impact.** If the isolated guitar on a real song contains drums, vocals or artefacts, the tone comparison will chase those instead of the guitar and [Gate 5](../../board/GATES.md#gate-5--beats-the-nearest-preset-on-real-songs-was-gate-3-then-gate-4-renumbered-2026-10-17-and-2026-10-18 "Gate 5 — beats the nearest preset on real songs (was Gate 3, then Gate 4; renumbered 2026-10-17 and 2026-10-18)") results will be unreliable.
- **Cause.** No real-song stress set exists, and the only separation model aimed at guitar has no stated licence so it was not used.
- **Resolution.** Run the separation stress test on a few real songs, locally, and record how accuracy falls as the mix gets denser.
- **Owner.** separation, after the owner supplies songs
- **Next step.** None: no separator work (human, 2026-10-25). Real-song separation quality stays unmeasured.
- **Evidence.** Separation run on the real song (no quality score); Separation stress curve on a synthetic mix (five densities)

#### Delay is the weakest effect: 5 of 12 exact models on the sealed set

**Low** · Confirmed Problem

- **Problem.** On [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)") attempt 3 (96 sealed items; all seven checks met on paper, but the human judged the model unusable and the gate is unsigned) the exact model was right on cab 12, eq 11, reverb 11, mod 10, comp 10, amp 8, drive 7 and delay 5 of 12. The seven delay misses: four named another delay model (i001, i049, i073, i089), three a different kind of effect (i041 a compressor, i065 and i081 an equaliser). Two ranking misses (i039, i095) are cabinets.
- **Impact.** [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)")'s checklist is not what blocks it now, but delay is common in real tones and the model is wrong on more than half of the delay items. Real songs will be harder than this known-clean-recording test.
- **Cause.** Not yet diagnosed. Swell-type and ducked delays look similar to other delays at the settings drawn; v3 is now seen, so diagnosis must use the development sets.
- **Resolution.** Diagnose the delay misses on development data (echo time, feedback and mix versus model), then strengthen the time-based specialist; listen to the delay misses to see whether the swaps matter by ear.
- **Owner.** search builds the delay work; the human listens; the lead schedules
- **Next step.** None: delay is on hold (human, 2026-10-25: 'I don't think any of the songs have any delay in them').
- **Evidence.** [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)") attempt 3 on all 96 sealed synthetic-v3 items: all 7 checks met (rank-1 0.979 under the extended rule B); closed as superseded by the human on 2026-10-25, not signed; Decision log; Gate checklists

#### A few task-board lines are stale

**Low** · Confirmed Problem

- **Problem.** Some task-board and gate-checklist lines are behind the ledger: the [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)") checklist items stay unticked on purpose after a failed attempt, and older lines carry stale wording.
- **Impact.** Anyone reading only the board will misjudge progress; no product impact.
- **Cause.** Work moved faster than board edits.
- **Resolution.** The lead updates the lines at the next planning step.
- **Owner.** tone-matcher-lead
- **Next step.** Update the board at the next planning step.
- **Evidence.** Task board

#### Test settings became defaults without itemised approval: now pinned, approvals still provisional

**Low** · Risk

- **Problem.** Since 18 October several test settings became defaults of the song-match script by reuse, and several approvals were one-word answers to bundled lists. Fixed on 25 October: a file (data/approved-methods.json) lists what the human has ruled on, with quotes, and a test pins the song-match flags to it; knob fine-tuning is an option with the level knobs locked; the provisional loss and the drive families are no longer defaults. Still open: the bundle approvals of 25 October are recorded as provisional, and the final ranking by the listening measure is an interim default.
- **Impact.** Small now: the record says which methods are approved. The interim ranking default is exactly the instrument the first blind session questioned.
- **Cause.** Agents proposed correctly, the next task reused the proposal as its configuration, and the one after packaged it. The lead has recorded this as its failure.
- **Resolution.** Itemised approvals in the human's words from now on (adopted); the human can object to any provisional line.
- **Owner.** tone-matcher-lead proposes; the human rules
- **Next step.** None required; the human may rule on the interim ranking default when the stem validation question is settled.
- **Evidence.** Methodology audit, 2026-10-23 (independent, read-only; evidence classes, overstatements found); Project analysis, 2026-10-23 (lead): verdict, alternatives, ten decisions for the human; Decision log; Approved-methods file and its pinning test

#### Building whole chains from single blocks is not yet shown to work

**Low** · Unknown/Unverified

- **Problem.** Single blocks are matched first and whole chains are built from analysis: the kinds of block present, ordered by common patterns. The pattern prior now rests on 2,068 curated presets and shows strong order rules. But the block-presence detector passed its test only for the cabinet and for whether a gain stage is present; for delay, reverb and modulation it missed its bars. The chain builder exists and is tested only with fake engines.
- **Impact.** [Gate 5](../../board/GATES.md#gate-5--beats-the-nearest-preset-on-real-songs-was-gate-3-then-gate-4-renumbered-2026-10-17-and-2026-10-18 "Gate 5 — beats the nearest preset on real songs (was Gate 3, then Gate 4; renumbered 2026-10-17 and 2026-10-18)") (real songs) needs whole chains. Presence is used only as a soft hint, so every time-based block must be confirmed by rendering with and without it.
- **Cause.** Behind an amp and cabinet, single-block detectors call the extra block a drive almost every time; comparing against the clean recording cannot isolate a block that follows a non-linear amp.
- **Resolution.** Judge post-amp blocks against the chain's current best render, render a multi-block test set on Native, and run the chain builder on a real engine. Cabinet choice by deconvolution looks promising.
- **Owner.** search (builder), tone-metrics (detector), native-engine (multi-block renders)
- **Next step.** None now: the product chain is drive, equaliser, reverb and the chain builder is out of scope until the chain widens.
- **Evidence.** Chain-pattern prior built from 129 presets (aggregate statistics only); Preset-corpus chain statistics (category level only); Block-presence detector scored once on its test split (single Helix Native blocks); Chain builder design and fake-engine tests; Cabinet impulse-response library analysis (aggregate note, vendor-free); Decision log; Task board

#### What the Line 6 licence agreements allow for training is not recorded

**Low** · Unknown/Unverified

- **Problem.** The owner read the licence agreements but the findings are not in the repository.
- **Impact.** It decides whether models trained on Native renders may be published; it blocks nothing until the learning stage.
- **Cause.** Recording the finding was left to the owner.
- **Resolution.** Write the relevant clauses' conclusions into the licence notes.
- **Owner.** owner, then reviewer
- **Next step.** Covered by the owner action about the licence agreements.
- **Evidence.** Line 6 licence flags

Each milestone is a gate in the project plan with a written checklist and the owner's sign-off. No deadlines are set, so none are claimed.

| [Gate 3](#gate-3-blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18-next-milestone) (next) | Product-level | **In Progress** | 1 of 4 | pending |
| [Gate 4](#gate-4-matches-clean-recordings-of-real-pedals-new-human-2026-10-17-was-gate-3-renumbered-2026-10-18) | Product-level | **Not Started** | 0 of 4 | pending |
| [Gate 5](#gate-5-beats-the-nearest-preset-on-real-songs-was-gate-3-then-gate-4-renumbered-2026-10-17-and-2026-10-18) | Product-level | **In Progress** | 1 of 3 | pending |

### Gate 3: blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18) (next milestone)

**State: In Progress** · Product-level milestone. Show that the tool can name the right block and settings from the rendered output alone, without ever seeing the dry guitar track that made it.

- [x] A sealed set of single-block items exists: random model, random settings, rendered in Native from a random held-out guitar sample the matcher never sees.
- [ ] The pass bars and baselines are registered before the run, under the same no-dry-track condition. They were written down before the set was drawn but are proposed by benchmark, not approved by the human.
- [ ] Blind prediction meets the bars and beats every baseline on the sealed set.
- [ ] A short blind listen of the prediction re-rendered from the held-out dry sample agrees.

**Demonstrated.** One of four criteria evidenced. The sealed set gate3-v1 exists: 120 single drive-block items (60 product drive models x 2 random settings) played from the reserved third guitarist's clean recordings, seed 223001, hashes recorded, labels unread, verifier re-checks every file. The bars (G3-1 to G3-5) were written down before the draw; the human has not ruled on them and nothing has been scored. Earlier informal groundwork stays evidence class D (predictor-first drive matcher on 48 blind Helix drive targets, set drawn after the method was built, no bar).  
**Remaining.** The human rules on the bars; one frozen method is scored once on the sealed set against the baselines; then the short blind listen (at least 30 decided trials, 6 repeats). The method to score is the chain predictor, which has no result yet.  
**Blocking.** The human has not ruled on the bars. Scoring allows one attempt per frozen method, and the method that would be scored (the chain predictor) is still being trained. Limit: the 120 items share about 31 distinct playing windows.  
**Next action.** Human: rule on the bars. Then the lead launches one scoring run of a frozen method.  
Evidence: Gate checklists; Decision log; [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") sealed set gate3-v1 note; [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") set sealed: 120 items; [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") bars registered (proposed, not approved); Predictor-first drive matcher, blind on 48 Helix targets; Predictor-first drive matcher note; Methodology audit, 2026-10-23 (independent, read-only; evidence classes, overstatements found).

### Gate 1: both engines driven by code (end of week 1)

**State: Complete** · Foundational milestone. Prove both sound engines can be driven by code, and that a generated preset loads on the pedal.

- [x] Helix Native renders a test clip from code (smoke test passes).
- [x] The HX Stomp renders the same test clip from code on at least one operating system.
- [x] A generated preset file is accepted by HX Edit and loads on the pedal.
- [x] The shared engine interface is frozen and the control routes are documented.

**Demonstrated.** All four criteria are evidenced and the owner signed off on 2026-10-14. Caveats the owner accepted: the pedal result rested on one operating system and firmware 3.50 (the pedal has since been updated to 3.80 and its smoke test passes there); the pedal-load result was reported by the owner, not observed by an automated test.  
**Remaining.** Nothing for the gate itself. Windows and Linux pedal control remain unverified.  
**Next action.** None. The pedal is now on firmware 3.80 and its smoke test passes there; the remaining 3.80 re-checks (parity with Native) belong to the next gate.  
Evidence: [Gate 1](../../board/GATES.md#gate-1--both-engines-driven-by-code-end-of-week-1 "Gate 1 — both engines driven by code (end of week 1)") sign-off record; Native smoke test; Pedal smoke test, independent re-run; Generated preset loaded on the pedal (owner-reported).

### Gate 2: recovers known presets (end of week 3)

**State: Superseded** · Core method milestone. Show the tool can recover a known answer: given audio made from a preset we already know, its comparison measure picks the true preset and its search finds the right blocks and settings, at thresholds agreed in advance.

- [x] On the known-answer set, the tone-comparison measure ranks the true preset first at the agreed threshold.
- [x] Block types, amp and effect models, and parameter values are identified within the agreed accuracy.
- [x] A curve showing how accuracy falls as guitar separation gets worse has been recorded.

**Demonstrated.** Attempt 3 ran on all 96 sealed synthetic-v3 items (ledger [2026-10-18T15:46:15](../../ledger/runs.csv?plain=1#L1178 "benchmark · native · synthetic-v3 · block_f1 = 0.885417 (first of 8 rows at this time)") (8 rows)): all 7 checks met on paper. Category 0.885 (bar 0.85), model 0.771 (bar 0.60), settings error 0.101 (bar 0.15), ranking the true preset first 0.979, 94 of 96 (bar 0.95), model against the random walk +0.208, settings against the random walk -0.083. The ranking check used the owner's extended rule B, amended before the run; attempts 1 and 2 keep their old-rule figures. The separation stress curve is ticked from 2026-10-16. The human's verdict the same day ([DECISIONS 2026-10-18 model and method decisions](../../docs/DECISIONS.md#2026-10-18-human-recorded-by-lead-model-and-method-decisions-require-the-humans-approval-the-current-model-is-judged-unusable "2026-10-18 (human, recorded by lead): model and method decisions require the human's approval; the current model is judged unusable")): 'the model we created is garbage. Something is flawed in our testing and/or our methodology'; every lead-made method ruling behind the gate, including the admitted score and the pass claim, is treated as unapproved until the human reviews it. Still open: the confirmation run on a fresh sealed set with the attempt-3 configuration unchanged ([T152](../../board/TASKS.md#t152 "Gate 2 confirmation run on a fresh sealed synthetic-v4 with the…")) and the harness sanity checks ([T155](../../board/TASKS.md#t155 "Test-validity checks")). The independent audit adds that the admitted score changed between attempts, the model bar of 0.60 sits at the random-walk level, and the result holds only when the clean recording is known. Evidence class A on paper; disowned in substance. Closed as superseded by the human on 2026-10-25 (bundle approval of the lead's decision 9; the 2026-10-18 verdict stands; the confirmation run and harness checks were cancelled). The checklist is kept as a record and is not a pass.  
**Remaining.** Nothing: the gate is closed, not passed. The sign-off line stays unticked.  
**Next action.** None.  
Evidence: [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)") attempt 3 on all 96 sealed synthetic-v3 items: all 7 checks met (rank-1 0.979 under the extended rule B); closed as superseded by the human on 2026-10-25, not signed; [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)") attempt 2 on all 96 sealed synthetic-v2 items: 6 of 7 checks pass (ranking the true preset first fails: 0.9375 against 0.95); [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)") attempt 1 on all 96 known-answer items: 4 of 6 criteria pass (category accuracy and model non-inferiority fail); Baseline "middle of every knob", 96 items (provisional weights, before admission); Hybrid search development runs on the 16 development items (not gate evidence); Tone-comparison admission: second Helix trust set, test split scored once (96 items, 19 look-alikes each); Tone-comparison tuning on the train split only (five stages); Native setting check, baked values: zero violations; Native setting check, repeatability, after the engine fixes: 216 of 216 renders within the line, worst -161.5 dB (the first run was 215 of 216, worst -97.4 dB); Native setting check, setting truth: 116 of 120 pairs, rule 2b 63 of 73; Tone-comparison trust check on real Helix renders (40 known chains, 2,400 renders); Separation stress curve on a synthetic mix (five densities); Gate checklists; Decision log; Methodology audit, 2026-10-23 (independent, read-only; evidence classes, overstatements found); Blind rating session v3: hard same-performance pairs (learned measure 18 of 25; admitted spectral measure 10 of 25).

### Gate 4: matches clean recordings of real pedals (new, human 2026-10-17; was Gate 3, renumbered 2026-10-18)

**State: Not Started** · Product-level milestone. Show that the tool can match clean recordings of real guitar pedals, judged by re-rendering the matched preset from a held-out dry take and by a short blind listen.

- [ ] A dry reference of the player's guitar (random playing) has been captured; it is the only dry signal the matcher may use.
- [ ] A set of single real pedals (one per available category, several random settings each) has been recorded, with the matching dry take kept aside for scoring.
- [ ] The matched presets meet a quality bar set before the run, and a short blind listen agrees.
- [ ] Two- and three-pedal chains have been recorded and matched by the chain builder at the same bar.

**Demonstrated.** Nothing yet on real pedals. Groundwork that carries over: the guitar calibration recorder and profile (one guitar), the dry-track rebuild, and a measured-pedal mode that is built and simulated only (model 96 percent in simulation); it has never been run on a pedal.  
**Remaining.** Capture the dry reference and the single-pedal set, choose the quality bar before the run, then record and match two- and three-pedal chains. The matcher must work without a lined-up dry take (note-invariant comparison terms, a learned guitar embedding, a phase-insensitive modulation score).  
**Blocking.** Depends on [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") and on the player recording the pedals.  
**Next action.** Wait for [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)"); the player can plan which real distortion pedals to record.  
Evidence: Gate checklists; Chain builder design and fake-engine tests; Blind A/B listening tool; Measured-pedal mode note (simulation only; never run on a pedal).

## What works and what does not

### Working and verified

| Capability | Current reality |
|---|---|
| Control Helix Native from code | Working on macOS with Native 3.82; a 2-second clip renders in about 0.28 s with memory steady. |
| Control the HX Stomp from code and restore it | Working on macOS, firmware 3.80; a whole preset is written, read back and checked, the pedal is restored exactly, and its blocks stay editable afterwards. Not yet checked: parity with Native on 3.80. |
| Read, write and check preset files | Byte-for-byte round trip on both reference files; a smoke-test preset loaded on the pedal (reported by the owner). |
| Catch a mismatch between what was written and what the pedal holds | Every parameter is compared after each write; this found a delay bug that is now fixed. |
| Native and the pedal agree on simple presets | Three of four tested presets agree closely; the fourth, with an added effect, differs more. Breaks for one cabinet setting. |
| Hold the pedal's effect models in Native as single-block templates | 605 of the pedal's 607 models captured (the other 2 are refused by Native 3.82) and every template replayed in a fresh host; delay times move in 10 ms steps. Measured on one Mac; nothing judged by ear. |
| Native stands in for the pedal on most effect types | Measured on amps, cabinets, dynamics, equalisers, most drives, delays, four reverb types, Hall above 500 Hz, tremolo, chorus, vibrato, flanger and phaser. The pedal still checks Ganymede-type reverbs, Hall on bass-heavy material, Bitcrusher-type drives and Early Reflections. |
| Render two variants in one pedal pass | A two-variant pass equals serial renders on firmware 3.80: path A -78.5 and -85.1 dB, path B -60.4 and -78.6 dB, each below the serial repeat floor, at about 1.7 s per variant (earlier: about 2.9 s). Measured on one pedal and two chain pairs. |
| Restore the pedal after a session, judged by decoded equality | The restored preset decodes identical to the backup. Raw bytes differed by 3 because the pedal re-encodes after another preset is selected, so the byte check is secondary. The offline re-check used hardware buffers. |
| Native applies the requested settings exactly and repeats renders | Repeatability 216 of 216 (worst -161.5 dB); zero unexplained start-up differences in 65 hosts; the in-run sentinel catches a forced drift with 0 unforced drifts in 9,770 renders. Setting truth 116 of 120 pairs; the misses are explained (one-step rounding, input/output blocks). One Mac, Native 3.82. |
| Write down the notes of a separated guitar part | Basic Pitch runs locally on all seven separated stems. Tuned settings gave note accuracy 0.30 against the human's own takes, up from 0.26, and the gain is small and unconfirmed. Stray notes remain. |
| Record and build a profile of one guitar | The human recorded the guided session (26 segments, no warnings): 263 own notes, a tone curve and a normal playing level of -36.1 dBFS. One guitar only. |
| Seal a blind test set before any method is scored | gate3-v1: 120 single drive-block items, hashes recorded, bars written down before the draw, a verifier re-checks every target; the scorer refuses to run without a launch reference. Nothing has been scored, so this shows the mechanism, not a result. |
| Pin song-match defaults to what the human approved | A file lists the ruled settings with quotes and a test fails if the song-match flags drift from it; bundle approvals are marked provisional. |

### Implemented but not yet proven

| Capability | Current reality |
|---|---|
| Pick a preset that matches a recorded tone | One song section tried; the score could not separate the top two choices; the listener's preference was informal. |
| Judge tone similarity reliably | No measure is approved by the human. The lead admitted one for single blocks with a known clean recording (0.990 on a held-out Helix split against a 0.90 bar), but the human judged that model unusable on 2026-10-18 and the admission is unapproved; on hard same-performance pairs that measure agreed with the ear 10 of 25 times (chance). The learned listening measure agrees with the ear on ordering effect models on the same performance (18 of 25, then 20 of 21 on the same experiment's picks) and nowhere else yet; never rated against a separated guitar. Without a known recording the earlier tests failed (about 7 of 40). Evidence class B at best. Update 2026-10-25: the first blind session against separated guitars answered with a verdict ('so bad I can't even compare them'), not ranks, so agreement on real songs is still unmeasured. |
| Isolate the guitar from a full mix | Runs in seconds; quality scored only on an artificial mix (21.2 dB guitar alone, 7.3 dB with a second guitar); not scored on a real song. |
| Choose a clean reference guitar recording | Picks from a library of 104 recordings; the benefit is unmeasured and the score varies a lot with the clip. |
| Search for blocks and settings | Hybrid search on all 96 known-answer items: right model 0.646, right category 0.802, settings error 0.132 on right-model items. Passes four of six [Gate 2](../../board/GATES.md#gate-2--recovers-known-presets-end-of-week-3 "Gate 2 — recovers known presets (end of week 3)") criteria and fails two; strong on drive, cabinet, compressor and delay, weak on modulation, reverb and amp. Synthetic known-answer data on Native; not real songs; the ear has not judged it. |
| Render a chain one block at a time | One 8-block mono test chain agreed to within 67 dB above 20 Hz; dual cabinets and stereo untested. |
| Measure each block's behaviour with a test signal | Fingerprints exist for 602 of 605 Native library models (24 settings silent by design, such as effect loops without a return); whether they improve model choice is not yet shown. |
| Record the player's own guitar alongside the song | Built and tested with fake devices; some takes exist; accuracy not measured. |
| Predict typical chain order | A bigram prior from 2,068 curated presets with strong order rules (drive before amp, amp before cab, delay before reverb) and super-groups. Aggregate statistics only; not yet used by a chain builder on a real engine. |
| Detect which kinds of block a tone contains | Scored once on a test split of single Helix Native blocks: only the cabinet and the presence of a gain stage pass; delay, reverb and modulation miss. Used as a soft hint. |
| Estimate a cabinet by deconvolution | Promising on a real clip when the amp is right (the pedal cabinet is the top match in 22 of 24); useless with the wrong amp. Not yet part of a search. |
| Build a whole chain from analysis | A chain builder exists and passes its tests with fake engines only; it has not been run on a real engine. |
| Pedal renders are sample-stable and settings land exactly | Not shown. Setting truth 20 of 40 and repeatability 12 of 17 on the pedal under the fixed rules; a one-sample slip occurs in about 1 render in 90; four models are refused. The longer pre-roll is accepted, but no pedal result counts until the slip is handled. |
| Rebuild a clean guitar from a separated song | Runs on seven songs; alignment to the stem is 0.29 to 0.46 on slow parts and unmeasurable on three fast riffs. The ablation (30 searches) shows the winning drive model mostly stays the same under other clean guitars (5 to 6 of 7 songs), so the rebuild's contribution looks small, but the distances are not comparable and the seven blind packs are unrated. |
| Match a song's drive effect from a rebuilt clean guitar | Seven songs searched across all 60 drive models; the human said they 'all sound pretty good' in one open pass, one listener, no alternative heard (evidence class C). One block stands in for the rig. The first blind listening on real songs (25 October, five songs, top-5 candidates) was negative: 'so bad I can't even compare them'. |
| Fine-tune the knobs of the chosen effect | The listening-measure distance fell on 6 of 7 songs and the human heard the same or better in one open pass (evidence class C). Output and Master were free and moved. The measure is not validated for knob moves (its invariance test says a small knob move changes it less than a change of clean guitar), and the human's 2026-10-18 ruling said knobs are not set by a sound-comparison score; this step is a test setting, not an approved method. |
| Name an effect from its output in slots after drive | Trained, not verified. On single blocks: strong on 18 held-out recordings of seen players, weak on a new player. Inside a finished chain the predictors fail (drive 2 percent, amp 1 percent exact model; knobs at the level of a constant guess). Evidence class D. |
| Learned predictor for good starting points | The drive predictor narrowed 48 blind Helix targets to 85 percent right model first and 100 percent in three; on the human's own takes 67 and 88 percent (not statistically significant). Evidence class D: the set was drawn after the method was built, no bar, no listen. Slot predictors are trained, not used by any matcher, and fail inside chains. |
| Identify a real pedal by probing it (measured-pedal mode) | Built and simulated only: model 96 percent, Drive 85, Tone 90 in simulation. It has never been run on a pedal. |
| Tune a whole amp chain by search | One song, one window: a tuned version of a tutorial chain beat the tutorial in a short blind session (8 trials, 6 decided, 1 of 2 repeats consistent) and one live listen. Evidence class B plus C; directional, not a demonstration that chains can be tuned reliably. |
| Run blind listening sessions | The session tool is built and seven short sessions (6 to 28 trials each) were rated for drive matching and one chain tuning. None covered the seven song matches, and the human's consistency on repeated pairs is 7 of 12, so the sessions are directional only. |
| Estimate a reverb's decay and amount from a separated guitar | Accurate on 189 known reverbs made in Helix Native (decay ordering 36 of 40 triples, rank correlation 0.85 to 0.89 on five songs; mix 0.88 to 0.94); cannot tell Room from Plate from Hall. On real stems a tail is readable on 2 of 7; the other five read separator leftovers. Confirmed blind on Mother, not reproducible on Territorial, pooled not confirmed. |
| Colour the drive match with a light reverb and a fitted equaliser | A light reverb after the drive was preferred on all seven songs in open listening; the fitted equaliser variants were indistinguishable but kept; one blind Mother pack favoured the fitted equaliser. No blind test of the reverb on the other songs, and Plate was not preferred blind. |

### Missing or not working

| Capability | Current reality |
|---|---|
| Turn a song into a pedal-verified preset with one command | Not implemented; the steps are run by hand. |
| Produce up to three tone snapshots from one song | Not implemented end to end. |
| Pedal control on Windows or Linux | Unverified; no machine available. |
| Stereo blocks and dual cabinets in Native | The Native host is mono only. |
| Look up song context | An opt-in lookup is designed and a 3-song live probe was reported; years and tags need a second lookup. Nothing is wired into matching. |
| Match amp, cabinet and delay as part of the chain | Out of scope for now (human, 2026-10-25): the product chain is drive, equaliser, reverb; an amp may stand in for the drive only as a candidate. The single-block predictors fail inside chains. |
| Match the product chain (drive, equaliser, reverb) from a song | Not implemented. The chain predictor's training data is still being rendered (37,998 chains at 2026-10-26 16:23); no result. |
| A reverb model per song | None. A fixed default was rejected by the human; the estimator covers a real tail on 2 of 7 stems. |
| A drive-only match that survives a blind listening on real songs | Not shown. The first blind session on five songs was negative. |

## Human decisions and actions

One list, each action once. Routine work owned by the agents is not listed here.

### Blocking now

- **Rule on the [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") bars** (High)  
  Why: Benchmark proposed five bars (G3-1 to G3-5) and sealed a 120-item test on the reserved third guitarist before drawing it. The standing rule is that you approve pass bars before they take effect, and you have not ruled on any.  
  Unblocks: Scoring the sealed set once with one frozen method, and counting the overnight chain predictor toward [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)").  
  Recommended: Approve, amend or reject each bar in your own words, one line each. Note from the set's author: the items share about 31 distinct playing windows, so an interval around 0.50 is wide.  
  If deferred: Predictor numbers stay unscored measurements and the product-level milestone cannot move.

### Needed soon

- **Rule on the reverb model: estimate when the tail is readable, fall back otherwise** (High)  
  Why: You rejected a fixed reverb default and asked for a model. The pre-registered pilot found no agreeing signal; the estimator is accurate on known reverbs, was confirmed on Mother, was not reproducible on Territorial, and reads a real tail on 2 of 7 stems.  
  Unblocks: A defined reverb stage in the chain and a clear comparison for the overnight reverb heads.  
  Recommended: Adopt 'estimate decay and mix when a tail is readable, otherwise fall back' as a test setting, not a default; the fallback value is yours to name (Plate was not preferred in the blind ranks).  
  If deferred: Reverb stays an open listening choice and the chain has no defined third stage.
- **Load presets on the Stomp and play them** (Medium)  
  Why: You said you want to test the calibrated matches yourself. No record shows the calibrated matches or the colour-pilot presets being played on the Stomp (the colour packs were built and not loaded); the ear on the real pedal is the final judge.  
  Unblocks: The first verdict on the real pedal for the calibrated matches and for the light reverb.  
  Recommended: Play each preset after its separated original, level-matched; say which version you prefer.  
  If deferred: The pedal remains unheard for these matches.
- **Decide whether to commit and push the accumulated work** (Medium)  
  Why: A large body of work (dry rebuild, calibration, fine-tune, slot data, [Gate 3](../../board/GATES.md#gate-3--blind-prediction-of-a-single-block-without-the-dry-track-new-human-2026-10-18 "Gate 3 — blind prediction of a single block without the dry track (new, human 2026-10-18)") set, colour and reverb pilots, governance file, report) exists locally and the public repository does not have it.  
  Unblocks: A saved, reviewable history; the public status page stays current.  
  Recommended: Commit and push after the lead confirms the full suite is green.  
  If deferred: Work stays local and unreviewed; a lost disk loses it.
- **Choose the first real songs for local testing** (Medium)  
  Why: Separation quality on real songs is unmeasured and the final test needs 20 songs. Pick a few now spanning clean to high-gain tones; the full 20 can follow.  
  Unblocks: The separation stress test and the song set for [Gate 5](../../board/GATES.md#gate-5--beats-the-nearest-preset-on-real-songs-was-gate-3-then-gate-4-renumbered-2026-10-17-and-2026-10-18 "Gate 5 — beats the nearest preset on real songs (was Gate 3, then Gate 4; renumbered 2026-10-17 and 2026-10-18)").  
  Recommended: Three to five songs now, local only; audio never leaves the machine.  
  If deferred: Separation risk stays unmeasured and [Gate 5](../../board/GATES.md#gate-5--beats-the-nearest-preset-on-real-songs-was-gate-3-then-gate-4-renumbered-2026-10-17-and-2026-10-18 "Gate 5 — beats the nearest preset on real songs (was Gate 3, then Gate 4; renumbered 2026-10-17 and 2026-10-18)") cannot be scheduled.
- **Record the play-along takes and report what happened** (Medium)  
  Why: The tool can record your own clean guitar alongside the song. The first takes exist, but the board does not say which commands ran on the pedal or whether the count-in and alignment were right. The clean guitar used strongly changes the score (7.7 to 22.7, lower is better), so this is the cleanest way to remove that variable.  
  Unblocks: Measuring the effect of the clean-guitar choice and the accuracy mode that uses your own recording.  
  Recommended: Record the DS-Sample section again with a guitar in the pedal's Main L/Mono input and note whether the click and alignment were right.  
  If deferred: The clean-guitar effect stays unmeasured; the search keeps using a library recording.
- **Provide about 130 songs with known artist and title for the song-context test** (Medium)  
  Why: Song lookups (MusicBrainz, opt-in, artist and title only) were approved. A live probe found 3 of 3 songs, but years and tags need a second lookup, so the larger test needs a list with reliable names.  
  Unblocks: Measuring whether artist and title give a useful prior for the chain builder and search.  
  Recommended: A plain list of artist and title pairs; no audio is needed.  
  If deferred: The song-context idea stays unmeasured; nothing else depends on it.
- **Rate the dry-ablation packs and more reverb ranking packs on other songs** (Medium)  
  Why: The seven dry-ablation packs are built with sealed keys and unheard; only your ear can say whether the clean-guitar choice changes the sound. The reverb estimator has a blind test on only two songs, one not reproducible.  
  Unblocks: Whether the rebuilt clean guitar earns its place, and whether the estimator holds on more songs.  
  Recommended: Rate the packs before reading the picks table. Reverb packs for more songs will be built with the estimator's pick named in advance, and a repeat clip included so your consistency can be reported first.  
  If deferred: The rebuild stays an unmeasured hypothesis and the estimator stays one song confirmed.
- **Choose another section of Cherub Rock for judging reverb** (Medium)  
  Why: You said the clip was not a good section for judging, so it was left out and only six songs were ranked.  
  Unblocks: A seventh reverb ranking.  
  Recommended: Name a section, or say to drop the song.  
  If deferred: Six songs stay the sample.

### Later / optional

- **Supply chord lists for other songs (optional)** (Low)  
  Why: A chord list made the rebuild more decisive on Mother, but you preferred the plain rebuild by ear.  
  Unblocks: Nothing needed; it keeps the option open for songs with unclear transcriptions.  
  Recommended: Skip unless a song's plain rebuild sounds wrong.  
  If deferred: No consequence.
- **Check the delay settings on the pedal after loading a regenerated preset** (Low)  
  Why: A bug that made the delay too wet was fixed, and the pedal now reads delay Time and Mix back exactly after a write. A look at the pedal screen would add a human confirmation.  
  Unblocks: Trust in the pedal read-back step that the final check depends on.  
  Recommended: Load the regenerated high-gain test preset and read the delay Time, Feedback and Mix on the pedal screen.  
  If deferred: No consequence; the read-back already covers it.
- **Provide a Windows and a Linux machine, or accept macOS only** (Low)  
  Why: Pedal control is verified on macOS only.  
  Unblocks: Cross-platform claims.  
  Recommended: Decide when the pipeline works; macOS-only is acceptable until then.  
  If deferred: The tool is documented as macOS-only for pedal mode.
- **Record what the Line 6 licence agreements say about training and automation** (Low)  
  Why: The reading was done but the findings are not written down. The owner decided on 2026-10-17 that trained weights may be published; the recorded licence findings are the backing for that.  
  Unblocks: The learning stage, which is not near.  
  Recommended: Add the findings to the licence notes in a few lines.  
  If deferred: Learning cannot start safely, but nothing earlier depends on it.
- **Confirm the copyright line in the project licence** (Low)  
  Why: The project is MIT licensed but the copyright holder line is unconfirmed.  
  Unblocks: A clean public release.  
  Recommended: Confirm or edit the name.  
  If deferred: None before release.
- **Open newly generated presets in HX Edit and report errors** (Low)  
  Why: Only HX Edit can confirm the file is accepted; an error code -8309 means the wrong device.  
  Unblocks: Confidence that generated files import cleanly.  
  Recommended: Do it for the final candidate presets only, not every experiment.  
  If deferred: Import problems would be found later.
- **Read the Discogs terms if Discogs is wanted as a second source** (Low)  
  Why: The approved source is MusicBrainz only. Discogs would add artist-gear detail, but its terms have not been read for this use.  
  Unblocks: A possible second metadata source.  
  Recommended: Only read them if MusicBrainz proves too thin.  
  If deferred: None; MusicBrainz is enough to start.
- **Rate blind sessions for the final song test when they exist** (Low)  
  Why: [Gate 5](../../board/GATES.md#gate-5--beats-the-nearest-preset-on-real-songs-was-gate-3-then-gate-4-renumbered-2026-10-17-and-2026-10-18 "Gate 5 — beats the nearest preset on real songs (was Gate 3, then Gate 4; renumbered 2026-10-17 and 2026-10-18)") ends in a blind rating by you; the sessions do not exist yet.  
  Unblocks: [Gate 5](../../board/GATES.md#gate-5--beats-the-nearest-preset-on-real-songs-was-gate-3-then-gate-4-renumbered-2026-10-17-and-2026-10-18 "Gate 5 — beats the nearest preset on real songs (was Gate 3, then Gate 4; renumbered 2026-10-17 and 2026-10-18)") sign-off.  
  Recommended: Nothing now.  
  If deferred: None.
